Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Hypersexuality: Significantly increased interest in or activity regarding sex.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Purposeless non-goal-directed activity, such as pacing or hand-wringing, driven by an inability to sit still.\n  Duration: 1 weeks\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - I can't quite remember if I was sleeping less or if my sleep stayed the same.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of at least 2 weeks when you felt very depressed, with low mood or loss of interest, and the voices or beliefs were still present even after your mood improved?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of feeling depressed or losing interest in things. My mood has only ever been normal or on the high side, but I definitely still hear the voices and have those beliefs during the times when my mood is completely stable."

Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Hypersexuality: Significantly increased interest in or activity regarding sex.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Purposeless non-goal-directed activity, such as pacing or hand-wringing, driven by an inability to sit still.\n  Duration: 1 weeks\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 8 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 8 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Delusions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that there have indeed been times when your unusual beliefs or perceptions (like hearing voices or feeling monitored) continued for a couple of weeks or more, even during periods when your mood had completely stabilized and you weren't feeling unusually high, energetic, or depressed."

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Lately, I've been experiencing some really strange things that I can't explain. I keep having these intense thoughts and beliefs that other people tell me aren't true, but they feel completely real to me. It feels like there are things going on behind the scenes that only I can see.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had periods of feeling unusually high or energized, or periods of feeling very depressed, around the same time as these beliefs?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. I've had periods lasting about a week where I feel incredibly high, energized, and hyperactive, like I'm constantly on the go. These intense, high-energy phases definitely happen at the same time as those beliefs I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when the beliefs or unusual perceptions continued even when your mood was back to normal and you were not feeling unusually high or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. There have been times lasting for at least two weeks where my mood felt completely stable and normal, but those strange beliefs and voices still didn't go away. Even when I wasn't feeling high or energized at all, the feeling of being monitored was still there.\n\nDoctor: How long have the mood episodes lasted compared with the times when the beliefs or voices were present?\n\nPatient: I've been dealing with these struggles for about a year and eight months in total, and those high-energy mood phases have been present for most of that time. I’d like to ask one more thing: during the times you felt unusually high or energized, did you also have decreased need for sleep, racing thoughts, or impulsive behavior?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I don't recall having a decreased need for sleep or racing thoughts during those periods. I also don't think I was engaging in any impulsive behaviors when my energy was high.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you still need your usual amount of sleep, or did you sleep much less and feel fine?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I'm not really sure and haven't paid close attention to my sleep patterns during those times. I can't quite remember if I was sleeping less or if my sleep stayed the same.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of at least 2 weeks when you felt very depressed, with low mood or loss of interest, and the voices or beliefs were still present even after your mood improved?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of feeling depressed or losing interest in things. My mood has only ever been normal or on the high side, but I definitely still hear the voices and have those beliefs during the times when my mood is completely stable.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when the beliefs or unusual perceptions continued even when your mood was back to normal and you were not feeling unusually high or depressed?"

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Hypersexuality: Significantly increased interest in or activity regarding sex.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Purposeless non-goal-directed activity, such as pacing or hand-wringing, driven by an inability to sit still.\n  Duration: 1 weeks\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 8 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Hypersexuality: Significantly increased interest in or activity regarding sex.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Purposeless non-goal-directed activity, such as pacing or hand-wringing, driven by an inability to sit still.\n  Duration: 1 weeks\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 8 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Delusions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that you have experienced periods where your unusual beliefs or perceptions (like feeling monitored or hearing things) persisted for a couple of weeks even when your mood had returned to normal and you weren't feeling particularly high, energetic, or depressed."

Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
